Amazon is taking orders for the Moto Tag 3 before it’s even been announced
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-11-23
13:09:32 UTC
22:09:32 KST

Motorola’s unannounced Moto Tag 3 has been listed for sale online, just five months after the Moto Tag 2 went on sale in the US, and before any official announcement of a follow-up. The listing on Amazon UK was spotted by X user @matteventu, and shows the tracker in a “Pantone Lucent White” color option, with a price of £24.96 (about $33) and a 1-4 month shipping estimate.
Amazon is taking orders for the Moto Tag 3 before it’s even been announced
The Amazon UK listing for Motorola’s new tracking tag has been live since Friday.
A second image shared by leaker Evan Blass also shows a vibrant purple version of the new tracking tag, which is described as “Pantone Indigo Blue” on a separate Amazon Italy listing. Both are new color options, with the Moto Tag 2 only currently available in orange or a grey-ish beige. While the Moto Tag 3 name suggests this is a follow-up to the Moto Tag 2, there don’t appear to be any major hardware differences between the two trackers.
Both use a replaceable CR2032 battery, claim to provide more than 500 days of battery life, and support UWB in Android Find Hub, among other similarities. The XT2561-1 model number seen on the back of the Moto Tag 3 images also matches that of the Moto Tag 2. That could mean that the Moto Tag 3 name is an error and these are just new color options for the Tag 2.
We’ve asked Motorola for clarification and will update if we hear back.

Anthropic's Safety-Conscious Corporate Culture Still Envisions Superintelligent AI
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-11-23
12:34:00 UTC
21:34:00 KST

Anthropic's Safety-Conscious Corporate Culture Still Envisions Superintelligent AI (msn.com) 8
			
		 	
				The Washington Post looks at Anthropic's "unique" corporate culture, where CEO Dario Amodei "leads his workforce through essays, long memos posted in Slack and twice-monthly all-hands talks jokingly known as 'Dario Vision Quests'...
"The CEO's output has established a constellation of beliefs treated as obvious, urgent and righteous inside the company: AI that supersedes humans is inevitable, the United States must limit China's AI development, and Claude might be conscious."
Inside this tight-knit culture, Anthropic staffers, who call themselves Ants, get to feel that they are working on the world's most urgent problems, with the smartest people, in a shared mission to safeguard humanity, according to interviews with more than a dozen people familiar with the company's ethos, including several current and former employees. At its core is a belief that Anthropic — and Amodei — know what's best for the world...   "A pervasive thing at Anthropic is: We're going to put on our capes and we're going to be the responsible ones, and we're going to accrue great power, but ... because we're doing it responsibly, it's responsible to acquire more power," said a former employee who left the company this year and, like others, spoke on the condition of anonymity to avoid retaliation from the company....
Its message to investors fit with a vision of the future that Amodei often sketches out for his staff, people familiar with the company said, in which AI companies have the power of nation-states.  After nearly six years of telling his workers that powerful AI will force society's leaders to makep difficult decisions, Amodei is now one of the powers who must decide how to act, in a major test of the corporate culture he has created....   [Amodei] set out to create a more safety-conscious culture when he co-founded Anthropic in 2021 with six people from his inner circle, most of them from OpenAI.   In an internal memo the following year, Amodei laid out the stakes. "The current trajectory of AI development makes me think that there's a 20 to 30 percent chance that AGI will lead to the destruction or permanent stunting of humanity," he wrote, referring to the concept of artificial general intelligence, previously unreported court records show. He added that truly capable AI "seems more powerful/dangerous than nuclear weapons." 
Amodei was careful about those he hired to work on that existential challenge. Recruits were screened with a "culture interview," even for back office and policy roles, according to former employees and an archived copy of the company's careers page... It's "a bit of an 'are you ready for the cult' interview," an Anthropic hiring manager texted the unsuccessful candidate, according to a screenshot reviewed by The Post...
Before ringing in Anthropic's record initial public offering at Times Square in New York on Thursday, Amodei delivered a short speech with some of his greatest hits.  "To state it unequivocally, we will pace the training and deployment of AI systems, taking as long as is necessary to have confidence that they are safe and will remain under human control," Amodei said.

Win4wine – Run Windows Shell as the Linux Desktop on Wine
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-11-23
12:28:11 UTC
21:28:11 KST

Linux のデスクトップを、Windows XP のシェルで。
日本語 · English
Important
個人の実験的なプロジェクトです。 日常の作業環境として使えるほど完成してはいません。 動作を確かめているのは、新しくインストールした Debian 13 に導入スクリプトで入れた場合だけです。 すでに使っている環境（ほかのデスクトップ環境やディスプレイマネージャが入っているものなど）への導入は試していません。 試すときは、仮想マシンや試験用のマシンをおすすめします。
Windows XP の explorer.exe を、パッチを当てた Wine の上で Linux のデスクトップシェルとして動かすプロジェクトです。
タスクバー、スタートメニュー、デスクトップ、フォルダウィンドウは XP 本物のものです。Linux のアプリも、XP の枠とタスクバーで扱えます。
Microsoft のファイルは同梱していません。お手元の XP の ISO から、必要なファイルだけを取り出して使います。
XP の ISO が無い方は、XP のシェルの代わりに ReactOS のシェル（Windows 2000 風のクラシックの見た目）でも 試せます。ReactOS は自由なソフトウェアなので、導入スクリプトが公式のリリースから取得します（docs/reactos.md）。
Windows 7 の ISO があれば、Windows 7 のシェル（Windows 7 ベーシックの見た目）でも試せます。まだ実験的な試作です（docs/windows7.md）。
- XP のシェル: Luna（またはクラシック）のタスクバー、スタートメニュー、デスクトップ、マイ コンピュータ、フォルダウィンドウ。
- Linux アプリとの統合: Linux のウィンドウに XP の枠が付き、タスクバー、Alt+Tab、最小化・最大化が XP と同じように動きます。 Linux のアプリは「すべてのプログラム」に並びます。
- 通知領域: Linux のトレイアイコン（StatusNotifierItem と XEmbed）が XP の通知領域に出ます。 XP 自身のネットワーク・バッテリー・音量のアイコンと、そのフライアウト（音量、電源メーター、ワイヤレス ネットワーク接続の状態）も 出ます。NetworkManager と PipeWire / PulseAudio の状態から作ります（AshesTom さんのフォークの XP Explorer から）。 ワイヤレス ネットワークの一覧から、初めてのネットワークにも XP のネットワーク キーの画面で接続できます。メニューや画面の文字は ISO の XP のファイルから読みます。
- XP Explorer からの追加: フォルダの左のタスク ペイン、画面のプロパティ（Windows XP とクラシックのテーマ、スタイルと配色。
配色は ISO の HIVEDEF.INF と themeui.dll から作ります）、画像の縮小版とプレビュー、XP のサウンド（起動、ログオン、ログオフも）、
ネットワーク接続、Internet Explorer 6、Windows Media Player 9、スタートメニューの左の列（インターネット、電子メール、
よく使うプログラム）。どれもお手元の ISO のファイルで動きます（prefix/extras.py）。
- キーボードの配列: 最初は Linux のシステムの配列（localectl）です。コントロール パネルの「地域と言語のオプション」→
「言語」→「詳細」（XP 自身のもの）で入力言語を足したり既定を変えたりすると、セッションの配列（Wayland では labwc、
X11 では setxkbmap）がすぐに従います。2 つ以上なら XP と同じ左 Alt+Shift で切り替わります（session/keyboard.py）。
- 日本語入力: fcitx5 + Mozc です。Ctrl+Space（または全角/半角）で切り替えます。GTK、Qt、Wine のアプリで使えます。
- 日本語版と英語版の XP: どちらの ISO でも動きます。言語は ISO から自動で判断し、字体（英語版は Tahoma など）、 ロケール、ようこそ画面をその XP に合わせます（英語版では日本語入力は設定しません）。
- Windows のアプリ: WoW64 の Wine 11.0 で、32bit と 64bit のアプリが動きます。端末で wine setup.exe と打つと、
インストーラーが作るショートカットは XP のスタートメニューに入ります。
- XP のようこそ画面: LightDM の greeter として、XP のようこそ画面とログオフの画面を出せます（任意）。 シェルが起動するまでは XP と同じ大きな「ようこそ」の画面を出します。
- XP のキー操作: Windows キー、Ctrl+Esc、Win+R、Win+E が使えます。
- クイック起動: タスクバーの右クリック →「ツール バー」→「クイック起動」で出せます（XP と ReactOS。Windows 7 は実機と同じく 「新規ツール バー」でクイック起動のフォルダを選びます）。スタートメニューから Linux のアプリのショートカットも置けます。 「デスクトップの表示」も実機と同じく、もう一度押すと窓が元に戻ります。
- 画面の拡大（DPI）: XP と同じ「DPI 設定」で、文字や部品を大きくできます（96・120・144 など）。
- 落ちても戻れる: XP のシェルが繰り返し落ちると、Wine 標準の explorer と xterm に切り替わります。
- Wayland のセッション（任意・X11 のセッション以上に実験的）: X11 のウィンドウマネージャーの代わりに、パッチを当てた labwc で 動かすセッションです（XP、ReactOS、Windows 7 のシェル）。モニターごとの倍率や 125%・150% などの倍率が使え、シェルはぼけずに描かれます。 Wayland のアプリにもシェルの枠が付きます。作ったばかりで、確かめた範囲は X11 のセッションより狭く、既知の制限もあります（docs/wayland.md）。 ふだん試すなら X11 のセッションをおすすめします。
- ReactOS のシェル（任意）: XP の ISO の代わりに ReactOS 0.4.16 の explorer で動かすセッションです。日本語と英語に対応します。 XP のシェルと並べて入れられます。
- Windows 7 のシェル（任意・実験的）: Windows 7 SP1 x64 の explorer で動かすセッションです（Windows 7 ベーシックの見た目。 Aero のガラスはまだありません）。スタートメニュー、タスクバー、通知領域、フォルダの窓が動き、Linux のアプリもアプリごとに タスクバーにまとまります。確かめたのは日本語版と、その文字を英語の言語パックのものに入れ替えた英語版です。
- Windows XP Professional SP3（x86）の ISO（日本語版か英語版）をご自身で用意してください。
- 新しくインストールした Debian 13 に、ログインして使う利用者として入り、次を実行します。
git clone https://github.com/mikuta0407/win4wine.git ~/win4wine
cd ~/win4wine
scripts/setup.sh --media /path/to/WindowsXPSP3.iso --greeter
- 再起動すると、XP のようこそ画面からそのまま XP のシェルに入ります。
XP の ISO が無いときは、ReactOS のシェルで試せます（ISO は要りません）。
scripts/setup.sh --shell reactos --lang ja   # 英語なら --lang en
ログイン画面でセッション「ReactOS Shell (Wine, X11)」を選びます。
Windows 7 のシェル（実験的）は、Windows 7 SP1 x64 の ISO から作ります。
scripts/setup.sh --shell win7 --media /path/to/Windows7SP1x64.iso
ログイン画面でセッション「Windows 7 Shell (Wine, X11)」を選びます。
詳しい手順とオプションは 🔗 導入手順（INSTALL.md） にあります。Wine をソースからビルドするので、4 コアで 1 時間半ほどかかります。 テーマ（XP のクラシックなど）と文字の大きさは、後から変えられます（見た目と文字の大きさを変える）。
Warning
- Windows XP（と Windows 7）の ISO はご自身で入手してください。 このリポジトリにも導入スクリプトにも、Microsoft のファイルを 取得する処理はありません。ISO と取り出したファイルの扱いは、Microsoft の使用許諾に従ってください。
- 利用は自己責任でお願いします。 データの損失や不具合について、作者は責任を負いません。
- Microsoft とも Wine プロジェクトとも関係のない、非公式のプロジェクトです。
- 確かめているのは日本語版と英語版の XP SP3（x86）だけです。ほかの言語の XP も英語版と同じ設定で入れるので動くかもしれませんが、
一切試していません（setup.sh は警告を出して続けます）。
LightDM ─ XP のようこそ画面 (greeter/)
   └─ XP Shell セッション (session/supervisor.py)
        ├─ Wine 11.0 + 151 本のパッチ (wine/patches/)
        │    └─ XP の explorer.exe, shell32.dll, …（ISO から取り出したもの。ReactOS や Windows 7 のシェルではそれらのもの）
        ├─ bridge-wm: X11 のウィンドウマネージャー + Win32 の代理ウィンドウ (bridge-wm/)
        │    （Wayland のセッションでは、ウィンドウマネージャーの代わりにパッチを当てた labwc。wayland/）
        └─ fcitx5 + Mozc, トレイの仲介 (bridge-wm/tray.py)
仕組みや設計の詳しい説明は docs/ にまとめています。
- loss32: Wine の上の Win32 のソフトウェアだけでデスクトップを作る Linux ディストリビューションの計画です。 ReactOS の部品も使います。win4wine が XP 本物のシェルを Debian の上で動かすのに対し、loss32 は自由なソフトウェアだけで作ります。
- Windows 由来のバイナリには手を加えません。違いは Wine とその周辺で吸収します。
- Microsoft のバイナリを逆アセンブルしません。挙動は公開 API と、実際の XP の上で動かす自作のプローブで調べています。
- Microsoft のファイル、取り出したもの、Wine の prefix は、Git 管理外の localres/ と .local/ にだけ置きます。
- AshesTom さん（フォーク）: XP の画面のプロパティ、
サウンド、キーボードの配列、通知領域の XP のアイコン、スタートメニューの左の列、Internet Explorer 6 と Windows Media Player 9、
起動中のようこそ画面、タイトルバーのアニメーションと小さいアイコン、Arch Linux への対応、xp.sh など。
LGPL-2.1-or-later（Wine と同じ）です。対象はこのリポジトリのコードとパッチだけです。
ただし wayland/patches/labwc/ は labwc と同じ GPL-2.0-only、wayland/patches/wlroots/ は wlroots と同じ MIT です。
Windows XP と Windows 7 のファイルと、スクリーンショットに写っている XP と Windows 7 の画面の著作権は Microsoft にあります。
ReactOS のシェルで使う ReactOS のファイルは、ReactOS のライセンス（GPL-2.0-or-later、DLL の多くは LGPL-2.1-or-later）に従います。

====================================================================================================

The next circle of security hell
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-11-23
11:34:16 UTC
20:34:16 KST

A few years back I wrote “The modern packager’s security nightmare”. Have things improved since? On the contrary, I feel like we’re slipping down a well-oiled slope with no end in sight. Vulnerabilities are reported at unprecedented rates. A lot of people are doing everything they can to patch them up, which doesn’t necessarily mean they’re not making things even worse. Complex software involves complex trees of dependencies, and every branch on that tree needs tending to. When they stop being reliable, people further down the tree start taking more weight. And there’s a limit of how much a single person can do, so what started as an effort to resolve an issue upstream may quickly become a weakness.
Is software less secure these days? It’s tempting to say yes. I’d love to blame the corporate sector for egotistically dumping low quality software on us. I’d love to blame marketing-oriented people developing stuff with the sole goal of making a profit. I’d love to blame Rust for getting people obsessed about memory safety, and ignoring the kinds of vulnerabilities that aren’t caught by the compiler. Finally, I’d really love to blame slop. But the truth is, I don’t know. That’s really beside the point.
The point is that nowadays everyone and their chatbot became security researchers. Projects are being flooded with vulnerability reports. Even though pretty much every bug bounty was discontinued because of slop, people aren’t stopping. And even if we disregard truly invalid junk reports, there’s a lot left, and you have to deal with them somehow. You have to figure out how important they are, and how to fix them (or more likely, whether the fixes submitted are truly valid and don’t introduce regressions), or if you should reject them and why.
In the end, security is tricky. We’re seeing lots of bugs like REDoS, or other kinds of “performance bugs”. Are they really vulnerabilities? Well, it depends on the attack vector. And that in turn depends on how the software is used. Some scenarios are really contrived, and feel like “shell can execute dangerous commands if you type them in”. But then, you also have really serious issues such as “timing attack can be used to guess user’s secret”. You need to know a lot to make that kind of judgment, and sometimes your judgment doesn’t really match users’ expectations.
In the end, what can project maintainers do? Even if they have the expertise and the energy to judge, if they can “know better”, there’s likely a CVE assigned already. It may have absurdly high score. All security scanners will complain unless you fix it, and users will come asking or complaining, or attacking, or forking. I mean, technology can’t handle nuances. More than once I’ve been doing unnecessary security stabilizations over vulnerabilities that didn’t affect Gentoo (for example, because we already unbundled the vulnerable dependencies), to avoid false positives from security scanners. I’ve been also doing meaningless version bumps (with no real changes or no changes that applied to Gentoo) so that our packages don’t get reported as being “outdated”. It’s a hassle and it sucks, but it saves people from worrying.
I’ve said this before and I’ll say it again: most projects aren’t really prepared to handle security issues. And it’s not just about triage. It’s about having LTS or security branches, it’s about making proper security releases with minimal changes. Unfortunately, it’s quite common for security fixes to be combined with new features, deprecations, API changes. In extreme cases, people are caught with their metaphorical pants down, and suddenly need to make a security release in the middle of a major rewrite, or along with barely tested new code. And then, people downstream discover that they need to quickly deal with major API changes to upgrade past a vulnerable version. These things keep piling up.
What happens next? The community reaches a breaking point. People can’t keep up. You discover you can’t rely on others, so you work around them. You start bundling more stuff. Soon enough your workload goes beyond your ability. Software development becomes “every chatbot for itself”. The nightmare becomes worse, and the wheel keeps turning.

Nospeaker ─ Make your MacBook's trackpad talk using its haptic motor
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-11-23
11:31:54 UTC
20:31:54 KST

────────────────────────  a90.com presents  ─────────────────────────
    _   __ ____      _____  ____   ______ ___     __ __  ______ ____
   / | / // __ \    / ___/ / __ \ / ____//   |   / //_/ / ____// __ \
  /  |/ // / / /    \__ \ / /_/ // __/  / /| |  / ,<   / __/  / /_/ /
 / /|  // /_/ /    ___/ // ____// /___ / ___ | / /| | / /___ / _, _/
/_/ |_/ \____/    /____//_/    /_____//_/  |_|/_/ |_|/_____//_/ |_|
─  haptic voice synthesis  ─────────────────────────────  trackpad  ─
Mute your Mac. Your trackpad will still talk.
There's no speaker in a MacBook trackpad, just a small haptic motor that taps the glass so you feel a click. NO SPEAKER drives that motor up to 4,000 times a second, fast enough to make sound you can hear, and turns it into a voice. It talks, sings, raps, and becomes an instrument under your finger.
One command. About a minute and a half. No speakers used.
[ mute your mac ]        it still talks; that's the point
[ put it on a table ]    on your lap it's much quieter
[ hands off the glass ]  until it asks you to touch it
Space skips a part. q or Ctrl-C goes to the end.
[ one line ]
curl -fsSL a90.com/nospeaker | sh
Downloads the latest release to ~/.local/bin/nospeaker and starts the show. a90.com/nospeaker points
to play.sh in this repo, so you can read what it runs first.
[ download ]
Grab nospeaker.zip from the latest release,
unzip it and run ./nospeaker in Terminal. Signed with a Developer ID and notarized by Apple.
[ from source ]
Needs Xcode or the Command Line Tools (xcode-select --install). Builds one program for Apple Silicon
and Intel Macs.
git clone https://github.com/os6/nospeaker
cd nospeaker
./build.sh
./nospeaker./nospeaker "hello there"            say anything
./nospeaker --voice Daniel "hello"   in another macOS voice
./nospeaker --sweep                  a rising tone, 100 Hz to 4 kHz
--dark                               white on black
--plain                              no visuals, plain text
A Force Touch trackpad (built into MacBooks from 2015 on, or a Magic Trackpad 2 or newer), and macOS 12 or later on Apple Silicon, or macOS 13 or later on Intel. Without a Force Touch trackpad, it says so and exits.
tested on
MacBook Pro (Mac17,10)   M5 Max   macOS 27.2.1   everything works
Not tested yet: Intel Macs, a Magic Trackpad, and older versions of macOS. MultitouchSupport is private, so older systems may lack the functions it uses, and loudness differs between trackpads. If you try it on another Mac, open an issue with your model, your macOS version and how it sounded.
say ──▶ formants ──▶ two tones ──▶ motor ──▶ glass
        per 10 ms    F1 + F2       haptics   sound
Why you can hear it. The trackpad has no speaker, but the motor moves the glass. Driven back and forth hundreds or thousands of times a second, the glass vibrates fast enough to make sound you can hear. It's quiet because the motor is built for short taps, not for sound.
The motor. MultitouchSupport is the private framework macOS uses for the trackpad. Its function
MTActuationCreateFromDictionary takes a description of a vibration, a list of sine tones with a frequency,
a strength and a length each, and MTActuationActuate plays it on the motor. Apple's public haptics API only
offers three fixed taps.
The range. Measured with the MacBook's own microphone, every semitone from 98 Hz to 3,951 Hz: how loud it is over a quiet room, and how much of the sound is the note itself.
│                                      ###
│                          #+#     +++#####        #
│                         +#+#+   .+++#####       .#
│              .       ++++#+#+   .+++#####    # #.# #
│   ++        +. . #..+++++#+#++++.+++#####+ #####.###          .#
│   +++#.     +...+#..+++++#+#++++.+++#####+ #####.####+        .#
│  ++++#...   +...+#..+++++#+#++++.+++#####+.#####.####+        .#
│ .++++#...++++...+#..+++++#+#++++.+++#####+.#####.####+  +#    .#
│..++++#...++++...+#..+++++#+#++++.+++#####+.#####.####+#.+#  .#.#
│..++++#...++++...+#..+++++#+#++++.+++#####+.#####.####+#.+#+..#.#
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
      131         262         523         1047        2093    3951
   public notes stop at 600 Hz ─┘
across: every semitone, in Hz · up: loudness, +16 to +34 dB
# clean note   + fair   . mostly buzz
It's loud from about 100 Hz to 4 kHz, with a few dead spots where the glass resonates. Its loudest, cleanest notes are around 1 kHz, which covers the most important part of a voice.
The voice. macOS's own voice speaks the line into memory. Every 10 ms, linear prediction finds the voice's two strongest resonances (formants), its loudness and its pitch. The motor plays those two resonances as tones, restarted on every beat of the pitch, so they buzz like a voice instead of whistling. Hiss sounds (s, f, t) become quick bursts of high tones. Two resonances beat three: in a blind test they were clearer, and louder, because the motor's strength is split fewer ways.
It sounds robotic, and it's much easier to follow when you can read along, which is why the show types every line as it speaks.
The code. One Swift file, nospeaker.swift, with no dependencies.
- Is it safe? It never drives the motor past its normal maximum strength. Going past it is possible and louder, but the driver starts to stall, so nospeaker doesn't.
- MultitouchSupport is private and undocumented, so a macOS update could break this.
The MultitouchSupport function names were learned from the open-source Tactile project. Everything else was measured on a MacBook.
──────────────────────  thanks for listening  ───────────────────────

Meta Muse can do your Black Friday shopping at Walmart
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-11-23
10:36:06 UTC
19:36:06 KST

Walmart will let Meta’s Muse shop on its website on your behalf, allowing the AI agent to find and purchase Black Friday deals. Tomorrow, on Tuesday November 24th, the retail giant will roll out a live implementation of the Agent Gateway it announced last month, which helps AI agents find relevant products for you without sharing your purchase history, according to Walmart.
Meta Muse can do your Black Friday shopping at Walmart
The Walmart Agent Gateway is about to launch with Muse support, with other apps and agents ‘on the way.’
Muse can make purchases through Walmart’s “Agent Profiles,” which provide a dedicated space for agent activity within your Walmart customer account. These profiles also allow you to set spending limits, approve purchases, and check the purchase history of everything bought by Muse. While other online retailers like Amazon and eBay have blocked AI agents from browsing their platforms, Walmart says that support for Meta’s Muse is “just the beginning,” and that additional Walmart shopping experiences for other AI agents and apps are “on the way.”
Here are Walmart’s instructions for shopping its website via Muse:
1. Tell Muse what you need. For example, ask it to find a great TV deal or show you which Walmart Black Friday deals are available for delivery. Muse can help you explore products and build your basket.
2. Set your shopping rules. Muse will take you to Walmart to sign in, then bring you back to Muse. The first time you add an item to your cart, you’ll set up your Agent Profile on Walmart within your Walmart account, including a spending limit you’re comfortable with.
3. Check out. Complete your purchase within the conversation using a card saved in your Walmart account, with the same Walmart prices you’d find on Walmart.com. And if something isn’t quite right, Walmart’s return policy still applies.
Update, November 23rd: Walmart informed The Verge after publication that the Agent Gateway launch has been delayed by 24 hours. The ability to shop on Walmart via Muse will now be available on November 24th.

GTA 6 building personal behavioral profile for every player
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-11-23
08:51:50 UTC
17:51:50 KST

GTA 6 building personal behavioral profile for every player
According to "make claude" (@make_claude) on X, GTA 6 is collecting player behavior data which is fine grained enough to estimate the following metrics: "impulse control score, risk tolerance index, aggressive response patterns, patience threshold, decision speed under pressure, spending impulsivity rating." Clients for this data include a "credit risk modeling company, human resources screening platform, and an advertising data broker."
Copy of the post as translated by X from Chinese into English:
Rockstar Games fired me on Wednesday. GTA 6 launched the next day
I was a senior engine engineer at Rockstar North, working on RAGE performance optimization. Three months ago, I found a data pipeline in the telemetry module that shouldn't have been there, and I could prove where it led
Right now, every running copy of GTA 6 is collecting player behavior data. That part isn't news. Every game collects telemetry. The privacy policy says "anonymous gameplay data analysis to improve future products." That's not what I found
The telemetry module's data feeds into an internal Take-Two system called Lens. Lens doesn't send anonymous usage statistics to Rockstar developers' dashboards. It builds a personal behavioral profile for every player. Not anonymous. Tied to your console account, your platform ID, your IP address
I reported this to my technical lead on August 14. He said it was "extended analytics for player experience research" and told me to leave it alone. I asked for the data processing protocol. He said it was handled at the Take-Two corporate level, outside Rockstar's scope of responsibility
I pulled the complete documentation for the Lens data pipeline. Here's what I found:
The telemetry goes far beyond playtime and crash reports. Lens records every input with millisecond precision. Keypress timing, stick movements, reaction speed, trigger pressure patterns on the PS5 adaptive triggers. It runs behavioral analysis in real time and generates what they call a "player behavior signature" - a profile unique to each player, just as a fingerprint is unique to each person
Lens infers the following from your gameplay: impulse control score, risk tolerance index, aggressive response patterns, patience threshold, decision speed under pressure, spending impulsivity rating. These aren't debugging metrics. These are psychobehavioral categories
It wasn't until I found the outbound data pipeline that I understood what they were for
This data doesn't stay at Rockstar. It doesn't stay at Take-Two, either. Lens sends the behavioral profiles to a Take-Two subsidiary called Vantage Analytics through an internal API. Vantage has no public website, no news page, no job listings. It was incorporated in Delaware in 2023
Vantage is a behavioral AI company. It uses player profiles to train predictive models and licenses those models to commercial clients. The pipeline documentation includes an incomplete client list. Two insurance groups, a credit risk modeling company, a human resources screening platform, and an advertising data broker
Your GTA 6 gameplay is generating a behavioral score right now. How fast you drive when no one's chasing you. Whether you stop at red lights. How you react when an NPC attacks you for no reason. How long you think before you act. How impulsively you spend in-game money. Every choice is feeding a model that companies are paying to query to make decisions about real people
And the original Chinese:
Rockstar Games 周三解雇了我。第二天 GTA 6 就发售了
我是 Rockstar North 的高级引擎工程师，从事 RAGE 性能优化。三个月前，我在遥测模块里发现了一条本不该存在的数据管道，而且我能证明它流向了哪里
此刻每一份正在运行的 GTA 6 都在收集玩家的行为数据。这部分不算新闻。每款游戏都会收集遥测数据。隐私政策里写的是"用于改进未来产品的匿名游戏数据分析"。我发现的不是这个
遥测模块的数据会汇入 Take-Two 内部的一个系统，名为 Lens。Lens 并不会把匿名使用统计数据发送到 Rockstar 开发人员的数据看板。它为每一位玩家建立一份个人行为画像。不是匿名的。与你的主机账号、你的平台 ID、你的 IP 绑定
我在8月14日向我的技术主管报告了这件事。他说这是"用于玩家体验研究的扩展分析"，让我别管这事。我要求提供数据处理协议。他说这是在 Take-Two 集团层面处理的，不在 Rockstar 的职责范围之内
我调取了 Lens 数据管道的完整文档。我发现的内容如下：
遥测的内容远远超出游戏时长和崩溃报告。Lens 以毫秒级精度记录每一次输入。按键时间点、摇杆动作、反应速度、PS5 自适应扳机上的扳机压力模式。它实时运行行为分析，并生成他们称之为"玩家行为签名"的东西 - 一份对每位玩家都独一无二的画像，就像指纹对每个人都独一无二一样
Lens 会从你的游戏过程中推导出：冲动控制评分、风险承受度指数、攻击性反应模式、耐心阈值、压力下的决策速度、消费冲动评级。这些不是调试指标。这些是心理行为类别
直到我找到了对外传输的管道，我才明白它们是用来做什么的
这些数据不会留在 Rockstar。也不会留在 Take-Two。Lens 通过内部 API 将行为画像传送给 Take-Two 旗下一家名为 Vantage Analytics 的子公司。Vantage 没有公开网站，没有新闻页面，没有招聘信息。它于2023年在特拉华州注册成立
Vantage 是一家行为 AI 公司。它用玩家画像训练预测模型，并把这些模型授权给商业客户。管道文档中包含一份不完整的客户清单。两家保险集团，一家信用风险建模公司，一个人力资源筛选平台，以及一家广告数据经纪商
你的 GTA 6 游戏过程此刻正在生成一份行为评分。没人追你的时候，你开得有多快。你会不会在红灯前停下。NPC 无端攻击你时，你怎么反应。你行动之前要想多久。你花游戏内的钱有多冲动。每一个选择都在喂养一个模型，企业正在付费查询这个模型，用来对真实的人做决定

Alice Huang In Touch With Chinese Spy Agency During Affair With Nvidia CEO, NYT Says (reuters.com) 18
			
		 	
				Reuters reports:
Alice Huang, the Chinese woman at the heart of Nvidia's recent scandal, kept in contact with an official from China's top spy agency for more than a year during her affair with former CEO Jensen Huang, the New York Times reported on Sunday.   Alice Huang was in touch with an officer from China's Ministry of State Security from the spring of 2025 until the fall of this year, the report said, citing people with knowledge of intelligence reports that describe the encounters... 
The scandal has also prompted a deeper look at Washington's approach to export controls on China, with lawmakers from both parties questioning whether the Trump administration has been too lax on enforcement and regulation of the prized semiconductors.
 
Reuters points out that several U.S. officials "have cast doubt on initial suggestions that Alice Huang was a spy..."
"A spokesperson for Jensen Huang declined to comment. Spokespeople for Nvidia and the Chinese embassy in Washington did not immediately respond to requests for comment. Reuters has not been able to reach Alice Huang for comment..."  But the New York Times also reported U.S. intelligence agencies have gathered evidence "that Ms. Huang's business partner, Kang Wei, who was often introduced as her husband, worked with the same Chinese spy agency."
American author/journalist Greg Palast has his own unconfirmed theory, speculating that maybe Alice Huang wasn't a romantic interest but the Nvidia CEO's "business conduit to the Chinese military industrial complex...  arranging the secret handoff of roughly one in five Nvidia chips."
Why did the two Huang's make the affair so obvious? If she's a spy, why flaunt it before TV cameras and paparazzi? Because Huang and Huang wanted you to know...  If Jensen and this guy's wife are conspiring to move hot chips to China, they can't exactly talk about it over the phone or in emails. So, they agree to meet-up in various hotel rooms during business conferences, doors locked...

Automating consumer debt collection with AI
About Us
CollectWise is a fast-growing and well-funded Y Combinator-backed startup. We’re using generative AI to automate debt collection, a $35B market in the US alone. Our AI agents are already outperforming human collectors by 2X, and we’re doing so at a fraction of the cost.
With a small team, we scaled to a $3M annualized revenue run rate in just over a year, and we’re now hiring a Founding Customer Success Manager to help us reach $10M+ over the next year.
The Role
We’re looking for a Founding Customer Success Manager to own customer relationships after launch, drive revenue expansion, and ensure customers are successful with CollectWise.
You’ll work at the intersection of customer success, account management, and operations, managing customer communication, resolving technical issues, optimizing AI agents, monitoring performance, and growing revenue from existing customers.
This is a highly hands-on role. You should be comfortable managing multiple customer relationships, proactively identifying opportunities to improve customer outcomes, making adjustments to AI agents and workflows, collaborating with engineering to resolve more complex issues, and growing revenue across your accounts by increasing platform usage and expanding customers into additional products and use cases.
Your success will be measured by customer satisfaction, retention, revenue expansion, product adoption, and customer performance.
Responsibilities
Own day-to-day customer relationships after customers have gone live, serving as their primary point of contact
Troubleshoot and resolve customer issues by adjusting configurations, coordinating solutions, and escalating technical issues to engineering when necessary
Drive revenue growth across existing accounts by executing upsell and cross-sell opportunities, increasing platform usage, and expanding customers into additional products and use cases
Monitor customer usage, performance, and trends to identify opportunities to improve results, increase adoption, and reduce churn
Continuously optimize customer AI agents, prompts, workflows, and outreach strategies to improve performance
Communicate technical issues clearly to customers and internal engineering teams, coordinating resolutions and keeping customers informed throughout the process
Build strong customer relationships through regular check-ins, performance reviews, and proactive communication, including preparing reports and presentations to demonstrate results and recommend improvements
Support new implementations, additional use cases, and product rollouts for existing customers
Assist with customer onboarding and operational requirements, including completing vendor diligence questionnaires related to security, compliance, and company operations
Desired Qualifications
Compensation
CollectWise is revolutionizing debt recovery with autonomous AI agents and an integrated legal network. We boost recovery rates, reduce costs, and maintain a positive brand image through respectful, data-driven interactions.

GrapheneOS Accuses Google of Kneecapping Its Android Partners Using Pixel Exclusives
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-11-23
06:06:00 UTC
15:06:00 KST

GrapheneOS Accuses Google of Kneecapping Its Android Partners Using Pixel Exclusives (itsfoss.com) 13
			
		 	
				Last year Slashed reported that the privacy- and security-focused Android fork GrapheneOS was "partnering with a major Android OEM to bring its hardened, de-Googled OS to Snapdragon-powered flagship phones." 
And early this November Motorola announced their upcoming high end Signature 27 phone would include official support for GrapheneOS as an OS option, the first Motorola device supporting GrapheneOS.
But the nonprofit GrapheneOS project has a new problem, reports the blog It's FOSS :
GrapheneOS has accused Google of leaning on phone makers to keep away from the project. The claim came in a thread on X [Thursday], where the project says makers were questioned about working with it, then threatened over their Google app licensing.... The project says Google is working against GrapheneOS itself and expects it to go further. It sees Pixel bootloaders being locked down eventually and Play Integrity, an API it calls illegal, being pushed harder. And if that were to happen, Android forks would struggle to survive under that.  
GrapheneOS believes some of Google's conduct breaks US criminal law and that antitrust violations can put individuals in prison. It wants to talk to the [U.S.] Department of Justice and says earlier cases against Google left its most serious violations largely untouched. Supporters are being asked for introductions to prosecutors and regulators, with assurances that the project has the necessary resources to carry on no matter what happens.  
GrapheneOS says more Android features and updates now stay Pixel only, leaving other hardware partners behind.

Yes, 17M lines of Lean is a lot
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-11-23
05:00:49 UTC
14:00:49 KST

Yes, 17m lines of Lean is a lot
The recent proof of the Riemann hypothesis came with a statistic attached: the proof is 17 million lines of Lean. Beyond the philosophical questions of what it means to have a proof longer than any human could comprehend, I’m left with a simpler question: is that as large as it sounds?
To investigate, I hunted for collections of Lean proofs online. I found two and charted the lengths of their proofs.
(All charts have a log x axis, which makes 17m lines look a lot closer to 1m and 100k than it really is.)
Famous theorems in Mathlib
Mathlib maintains a collection of famous proofs. There are over 1k but only 259 have Lean associated, which I chart here.
These are all human generated, and as we can see, they come nowhere close to 17m.
Every Lean package on Reservoir
Reservoir indexes every published Lean package, 938 of which have Lean code on GitHub. This one has a lot more data, including AI assisted proofs. We even have a few that are over 16m!:
- 88m: Qiuzhen-CFSG/CFSG this appears to be an AI built proof that is still a work in progress.
- 36m: CBirkbeck/LeanBridge, a project linking the LMFDB database of number theory objects to Mathlib. This isn’t really a proof: about 98% of its Lean is files generated by scripts that check the database’s numbers one by one. The hand-written part is closer to 500k lines.
- 27m: anthropics/fermats-last-theorem is another Anthropic special: a complete proof of Fermat’s Last Theorem, published on GitHub in September. Its README says “the Lean sources were produced by AI agents building on human-written open-source Lean”, including the human FLT project led by Kevin Buzzard, which is about 60k lines and still in progress. The AI version is roughly 450 times longer.
So yes, 17m lines is a lot.

A Partial Evaluator for C
France
Unknown Publisher
2026-11-23
05:51:48 CET
13:51:48 KST

Tempo Specializer - A Partial Evaluator for C
What is Tempo?
Partial evaluation is program
transformation that automates a program
specialization process. Tempo is a
partial evaluator for C programs. It has been applied in various
domains such as operating systems and networking, computer graphics, scientific
computation, software engineering and domain specific languages. A first
workshop on Tempo has been organized in March 1998.
Tempo is now being distributed. Its documentation
is available on line, as well as tutorial
slides.
An Overview of Tempo
Tempo is a partial
evaluator for C programs. It is being developed in the Compose
project at IRISA / INRIA - LABRI
(France).
Tempo is an off-line specializer; it is divided into two phases:
analysis and specialization.
The input to the analysis phase consists of a program and a description
of which inputs will be known during specialization and which will be unknown.
Based on this knowledge, dependency analyses propagate information about
known and unknown values throughout the code and produce an annotated program,
indicating how each program construct should be transformed during specialization.
Because C is an imperative language including pointers, the analysis phase
performs alias and side-effect analyses in addition to binding-time
analyses. The accuracy of these analyses is targeted towards keeping
track of known values across procedures, data structures, and pointers.
Following the analysis phase, the specialization phase generates a specialized
program based on the annotated program and the values of the known inputs.
Tempo can specialize programs at compile time (i.e.,
source-to-source tranformation) as well as run time
(i.e., run-time binary code generation).
For more details, please consider reading some of our papers, most of which
are available at the following URL: papers.
Program Analysis
As a first step, the user provides a program and specifies what arguments
(including global variables) are static. For example, the user provides
the following code for a miniprintf function, and specifies in
this way that the first argument is static whereas the second is dynamic:
miniprintf(S,D).
miniprintf(char fmt[], int val[])
{
  int i = 0;
  while( *fmt != '\0' ) {
    if( *fmt != '%' )
       putchar(*fmt);
    else
      switch(*++fmt) {
        case 'd' : putint(val[i++]); break;             
        case '%' : putchar('%');     break;
        default  : prterror(*fmt);   break;
      }
    fmt++;
  }
}
From this Tempo produces a program file with analysis annotations.
These annotations can be visualized using colors that highlight the propagation
of the known/unknown information throughout the program.
/*  LEGEND:  STATIC  DYNAMIC
 */
miniprintf(char fmt[], int val[])
{
  int i = 0;
  while( *fmt != '\0' ) {
    if( *fmt != '%' )
       putchar(*fmt);
    else
      switch(*++fmt) {
        case 'd' : putint(val[i++]); break;              
        case '%' : putchar('%');     break;
        default  : prterror(*fmt);   break;
      }
    fmt++;
  }
}
The blue color (bold face for black and white display) represent static
constructions, i.e. values that can be computed at specialization
time. The red color (standard font for black and white display) is for
dynamic expressions, whose value cannot be precomputed knowing only the
static arguments. Basically, everything in blue (bold) will disappear after
specialization; only red (standard font) parts will remain.
The visualization of the analysis is very important for the user to
assess the amount of specialization in the code. Furthermore, the analysis
speeds up the specialization process (as opposed to
on-line partial
evaluators). The analysis is inter-procedural and takes into account pointer
aliases and side-effects.
Compile-Time Specialization
When the user is satisfied with the analysis (i.e., what the user
expects to specialize indeed appears as static), actual specialization
values must be provided. For example, giving
"<%d,%d>" as the
actual specialization value for the fmt argument of the miniprintf()
function yields the following specialized code.
miniprintf_1(int val[])
{
  putchar( '<'    );
  putint ( val[0] );
  putchar( ','    );
  putint ( val[1] );
  putchar( '>'    );
}
Many specializations can be made, sharing the same analysis. Only different
specialization values have to be provided.
Runtime Specialization
Tempo can also perform runtime specialization, using optimized binary code
templates. A dedicated runtime specializer is generated from the analysis
of the program. In the case of the
miniprintf function, Tempo
generates a runtime specializer rts_miniprintf_1() which can be
used as in the following example.
/*
 * Some dynamic execution context for variable 'format'
 */
spec_printf = rts_miniprintf_1(format);
...
(*spec_printf)(val1);   /* <=> miniprintf(format,val1) */
(*spec_printf)(val2);   /* <=> miniprintf(format,val2) */
The function rts_miniprintf_1() is a dedicated runtime specializer.
It returns a pointer to the specialized function. Several specialized versions
can also be generated and used at the same time.
Applications of Tempo
Tempo has been successfully applied in various domains, yielding substantial
speedups.
- 
Operating system and networking:
  - 
Sun RPC, Chorus IPC, BSD packet filter, Unix signals, active networks,
- 
Computer graphics (various filtering algorithms),
- 
Scientific computation (trajectory, FFT, math libraries),
- 
Software architectures and mechanisms:
  - 
Domain specific languages GAL, PLAN-P),
  - 
Selective broadcast (implicit invocation),
  - 
Pattern matching,
  - 
Interpreters,
  - 
Software layers,
  - 
Generic libraries.
For an up-to-date list of non-confidential, published applications, please
browse our ``papers'' web page: papers.
Besides C, Tempo is currently being used as a back-end for specializing
Java programs using Harissa, a JavaVM-to-C compiler.
Fortran and C++ programs have also been specialized using translators to
C.
Projects Related to Tempo
- 
Cyclone
- 
Cyclone is a mobile code system being developed as part of the
SwitchWare
active-network project at the University
of Pennsylvania. Cyclone uses two ideas to make mobile code safe as
well as fast: run-time code generation and proof-carrying code. Cyclone
relies on Tempo's analysis front-end (i.e. the binding-time analysis) and
on TAL/T, an extension of the TAL
typed assembly language.
Workshop
A workshop (actually closer to a ``winter
school'') on Tempo has been organized at IRISA,
March 16-18, 1998. The aim of this workshop was to present a three-day
tutorial on Tempo and its applications to real size programs. We had 24
participants, coming from universities as well as industries. We believe
it was a large success. The talks
are now available on-line.
Depending on request, if the distribution
of Tempo is a success, we might organize another workshop later. It would
less be like a ``winter school'' but rather like a real workshop with users
presenting applications and open discussions about possible improvements.
It would be preceded by an optional one-day tutorial for people not knowing
partial evaluation or Tempo.
Distribution
The Tempo specializer has been made publicly available under a licence
in April 1998. At of March 99, it was being used by about thirty people
around the world. The binary version of Tempo has just (July 2000) been
made available without any restrictions, and we expect a full unrestricted
source release under GPL to follow soon.
Getting access to Tempo no longer requires signing a license agreement.
Tempo is simply available via anonymous FTP, as described below. In any
case, we would very much like to hear from you if you are an active
Tempo user. If you are, please also let us know if and for what purpose
you are using Tempo, by sending an email to tempo@labri.fr.
We will by default add you to our tempo talk mailing list tempo-talk@labri.fr,
where future Tempo releases will be announced, and Tempo users can discuss
practical problems using Tempo.
Tempo can be downloaded :
- 
Here is a Tempo release that have been made publicly available:
i386 Linux
- Most recent Tempo binary distribution for i386 Linux (tested on RedHat 6.1).
(Please remember to send an email to tempo@labri.fr
to let us know who you are and why you are using Tempo. Remember to explicitly
indicate if you are not interested in joining the tempo-talk@labri.fr
mailing list.)
In case of any problem with getting or installing the distribution,
get in touch with us: mailto:tempo@labri.fr
References
Here are general papers about Tempo and its architecture. More focused
and up-to-date information regarding Tempo can be found in the main on-line
papers tank.

A Partial Evaluator for C
France

Tempo Specializer - A Partial Evaluator for C
What is Tempo?
Partial evaluation is program
transformation that automates a program
specialization process. Tempo is a
partial evaluator for C programs. It has been applied in various
domains such as operating systems and networking, computer graphics, scientific
computation, software engineering and domain specific languages. A first
workshop on Tempo has been organized in March 1998.
Tempo is now being distributed. Its documentation
is available on line, as well as tutorial
slides.

(France).

(i.e., run-time binary code generation).

are available at the following URL: papers.
